ƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â below means code exists and its stated local checks pass. It does not mean multi-client, provider, or UI runtime acceptance has been exercised.

## A. Repository audit

### Stack and boundaries

| Area | Observed implementation | Constraint / current evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Desktop IDE | Code OSS 1.144.0 source checkout; branded through `product.json`; extensions are built in under `extensions/` | Full `npm run compile` passed before the latest extension-only changes. Normal editor, terminal, debugger, Git, preview, and extension workbench are inherited from Code OSS. No full IDE smoke test was run in this pass. |
| Forge Room Studio | Plain JavaScript VS Code extension, six native theme contributions, theme gallery webview, palette preview, reversible application, validated JSON profile import/export | No Studio automated unit suite was found. UI behavior, contrast warnings and profile round-trip are source-based claims awaiting IDE exercise. |
| Team Lobby | TypeScript VS Code extension; `src/extension.ts` contains room host/client, task/note/history, IDE context, agent workflows, and Build review; pure helpers under `src/`; webview in `media/room.js` and `media/room.css` | `npm test` builds TypeScript and runs 43 focused Node tests. Webview checks are syntax-only. Extension tests are helper-level, not VS Code-host integration tests. |
| Room transport | `ws` WebSocket server/client in the extension host; optional Node HTTPS server for WSS, private invite tokens, participant identity pinning, 12-hour invite expiry, 32-participant cap | Self-hosted, host-process-bound room; plain WS is trusted-LAN only. No managed identity, shared durable service, or validated internet deployment. Protocol 11 is required. |
| Persistence | Host room events/tasks/notes in versioned JSON under extension global storage; optional user-consented, bounded Ask/Plan transcript and Build session state; task worktree mapping in global state | Room history cap 500 events/200 tasks. Build snapshot is unencrypted, capped at 750 KB and re-consented for model transfer after restart. Not a cloud database or cross-host recovery system. |
| Agent/model | VS Code language-model API used for provider-neutral Ask/Plan/Build; context assembled from selected editor/workspace sources; read-only search/read calls require consent; Build proposal is reviewed before apply | This dev profile has no selected provider. No provider-backed flow, tool call, cancellation, restart recovery or model behavior was exercised. No external agent runtime/ACP adapter exists. |
| Execution and authorization | Separate context/provider confirmation; request-scoped read-only tool approval; explicit verification-task approval; reviewed diff and apply; guarded checkpoint rollback | No direct agent shell/write/network execution adapter, general policy engine, OS sandbox, per-resource room revocation, or durable security audit service. Approval UI is not a sandbox. |
| Git and tasks | VS Code Git API observation, selected Git diff sharing, merge commit event, optional conflict-path sharing, task-linked local worktrees, verification task outcome | APIs are used through the built-in Git extension. Worktree and Git flows have not had multi-client/manual runtime exercise. Shared diff is capped at 8,000 characters. |
| UI and accessibility | Native workbench contributions plus two theme-token-aware webviews with CSP, focus states and reduced-motion support | No DOM injection into Code OSS workbench. No IDE window was available for visual QA; no drag/resize custom dock, live layout morph, voice controls or mobile UI. |
| Build and distribution | `team-lobby-ide-0.48.0.vsix`, Studio 0.9.0, built-in registration in Code OSS `product.json` | Team Lobby 0.48.0 package, mirrored sources, manifest version and SHA-256 match. No release signing, updater, marketplace service, or distribution/legal audit completed. |

### Requirement gap map

| Requirement | State | Main code path / evidence | Dependency and API constraint | Priority / risk | Acceptance test next |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Preserve full IDE | Inherited; compile-only evidence | Code OSS base checkout and `product.json` | Existing VS Code APIs are stable extension surface; extensions cannot modify workbench DOM or inject arbitrary CSS | V1 / low change risk; high regression impact | Launch branded IDE; edit, debug, terminal, Git, preview, extension install and restart smoke test |
| Multi-person room, presence, invite, reconnect | Partial local/self-hosted implementation | `extensions/team-lobby/src/extension.ts`, protocol v11, participant snapshot, ID-routed DM and invite revocation helpers | No account identity; invite is a bearer secret; host process owns room | V1 / high security and operations risk | Two isolated IDE windows, host plus two clients, duplicate names, private-message isolation/history, invite revoke/reconnect denial, persistence |
| Human and agent actors / room task flow | Partial | Room identity roles, participant-ID task ownership; host-side enforcement lets only the host or assigned owner update tasks, only the host can reassign/reopen; member cards display active tasks by participant ID; criteria, dependencies, task-linked Build events | Agent is currently a participant label; no separate runtime identity or service runner | V1 / medium | Host/client task state, unauthorized task mutation checks, owner and actor history. Protocol 10 adds participant-ID ownership; create writes are limited to open tasks assigned to connected members, updates require the current owner or host, and only the host may reassign/reopen. Helper tests pass; full room runtime is still needed. |
| Uncommitted code visibility | Partial | Explicit selected Git diff is previewed and confirmed before sharing; file activity sends relative path only | Does not stream unsaved buffers; only tracked changes in selected file | V1 / high privacy risk | Two clients verify only selected diff is received and saved; ignored/untracked content stays out |
| Code-anchored review comments | Implemented; runtime validation pending | `src/commentAnchor.ts`, protocol event `comment`, `Team Lobby: Comment on Selected Lines` | Shares comment, relative path and line range; source excerpt stays local; anchors can drift | V1 / medium | Comment from selection; second client opens at anchor; stale/out-of-range anchor degrades safely |
| Permission revocation / audit | Partial | `Room.revokeInvite` deletes the bearer token before closing sessions; active message handling checks token presence; bounded host history records revocation without the secret | Invite scope only; no account identity or per-resource capability grants | V1 / high | Revoke while connected; verify protected messages and reconnect are rejected, other invite remains live, history has no secret |
| Watch/follow shared editor | Missing | No shared selection/cursor/editor-follow protocol | Public extension API exposes local editor events; full remote state must be explicitly shared; no workbench DOM control | V1 / high privacy / medium feasibility | Follow file/selection without stealing local active editor; toggle and revoke |
| Shared editing | Missing | No document synchronization or CRDT/OT | Must preserve unsaved buffers and define conflict/undo semantics; APIs differ by editor | V2 / very high data integrity | Two-client concurrent edits, disconnect/reconnect, undo, conflict and local buffer preservation |
| Voice/video/call | Missing | None | WebRTC signaling, device access, NAT/relay, consent and encryption claims require dedicated service/operational design | V2 / high cost/privacy | Multi-network audio, device switching, mute/deafen, reconnect, explicit share status |
| IDE-aware AI Ask/Plan/Build | Partial implementation | `AgentWorkflows` in `src/extension.ts`; language-model API; plan editor/handoff helper; task-linked, attributed room activity for start, proposal, apply, cancellation/failure and verification | Provider/model API availability varies by host and user; no configured provider here; room activity shares task title and bounded counts/IDs, not prompt/context/code | V1 / medium | Provider-backed Ask/Plan/Build, cancel, denial, context audit, apply and restart recovery; two-client event and verification validation |
| Native tool execution / ACP | Missing | Bounded read-only tools only; no ACP client | ACP is an agent/client JSON-RPC protocol and may expose file operations and permission requests; each capability needs host policy and approval mapping | V1/V2 / high | Fake/test agent adapter; capability negotiation; denied tools; cancel; bounded output; no implicit shell |
| Skills-first reusable behaviors | Partial, implemented in Team Lobby 0.38.0 and carried forward | Workspace-local `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` discovery; validates required metadata and directory match; users select up to three skills per request; selected bodies appear in transfer consent; scripts and supporting resources are not loaded or run | Implements required metadata and folded/literal descriptions; project instructions remain untrusted and confer no tools | V1 / medium | Test selection and exact provider input with configured model; verify malformed/oversized skills are skipped and no script executes |
| Project memory / instructions | Partial | User-selected `AGENTS.md`, `.forge-room/instructions.md`; consent displays source paths and sizes | Content is untrusted project text; must not grant tools or change transfer policy | V1 / medium | Prompt-injection test in instructions; deselection and exact serialized-input provenance |
| Context grounding and provenance | Partial | Explicit editor/workspace context and provider-token preflight; likely secret paths hidden by default with additional opt-in; read-only search/read calls | Provider token estimate is now consented separately before generation, with an over-limit stop; estimates cover serialized prompt text and may omit tool overhead. Build review checkpoints now include bounded tool names, source paths, outcome, output size, and time without storing tool results or search arguments; aggregate budget across calls and live provenance display remain open | V1 / medium | Compare visible source set to exact provider request; redact/exclude tests; tool result origin links |
| Diff, checkpoints, verification evidence | Partial | Diff proposal/apply; ten checkpoint cap; rollback guards; review bundle export | Build review exports now include bounded workspace-tool provenance and up to twenty verification outcomes, including failures; they do not yet form a full event graph joining all tool calls, room events, and human review decisions | V1 / high data integrity | Apply/reject/partial failure/rollback with a user's intervening edit and accurately recorded test results |
| Morph prompt-to-layout/theme | Partial/basic theming | Studio theme gallery, palettes and profiles | VS Code extension APIs allow themes/settings/views but not arbitrary workbench DOM or bespoke window chrome | V2 / medium | Declarative allowlisted layout/profile preview, apply, undo, accessibility check |
| Widgets / Workshop / Packs | Missing | No generator, sandbox, manifest, install lifecycle, pack signature or rollback | VS Code extension installation is a broad code capability; widgets need restrictive sandbox and IPC boundaries | V2 / high supply-chain risk | Data-bound widget permissions, isolated preview/build, pack permission diff and uninstall/rollback |
| Forge Mesh / Pipeline | Missing | Room tasks/dependencies exist; no event graph, agent handoff protocol or scheduler | Skills are behavior instructions, not a scheduler/event bus; dispatch needs idempotency and execution ownership | V2 / high | Dependency validation, bounded retries, idempotent schedule, policy-gated dispatch and real test evidence |
| Forge Pocket / mobile | Missing | None | Phone remote actions require authenticated online service, step-up auth and server-side scope checks; local host is unavailable while PC is off | V2 / high security/ops | Phone joins, reads status, reviews diff; revoked or offline action fails closed |
| Countdown / hackathon readiness | Partial | `Room.sessionDeadline` is host-set, UTC validated, stored in versioned host-local history, included in join history and broadcast to connected clients; host-only control and live display | Host process is the authority; no pause/resume policy, reconnect clock-skew handling, reminders, checklist or durable shared service | V1 / medium | Verify host/member/two-client set, clear, join and reconnect; define pause semantics and readiness checklist |

## B. Architecture decisions (proposed; evidence and rollback kept explicit)

Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â Keep Code OSS as the desktop shell

Use supported extension contributions and VS Code APIs for Room, Studio, AI, settings, editor selection and tasks. Keep the editor as the main workspace and avoid direct DOM/CSS injection. A future fork-only native workbench change needs a separate ADR and accessibility/regression suite. **Rollback:** disable/remove Forge extensions and built-in registrations; Code OSS core remains intact.

### ADR-002 Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â Treat room data as explicit versioned events

Keep actor, timestamp, type, scope metadata and validated bounded payloads on room events. Add correlation and permission-decision events before enabling remote operations. A host-local JSON history remains prototype persistence; managed room service migration needs schema versioning and re-authentication. Do not transmit file contents by implication. **Rollback:** bump protocol for incompatible schemas and refuse older clients rather than partially parsing them.

Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â Separate observe, share, execute and apply

Use distinct grants for context transfer, room metadata, read-only tools, execution, artifact apply and remote control. Approval is scoped and expires; server validates each protected room operation. Worktree/patch preview and guarded rollback remain the file-change path until an isolated execution service exists. **Rollback:** revoke a grant, cancel the run, restore only files whose current content still matches the applied checkpoint.

Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â Integrate Agent Skills as instructions, not authority

Discover a standard `SKILL.md`, validate metadata and show skill name/source before use. Load only explicitly selected relevant skill instructions into the model request. Do not execute bundled scripts in the extension host, treat a skill as a permission rule, or claim skill instructions provide memory/security. Add tools only through a native capability registry with independent policy. **Rollback:** deselect/remove a skill; no persistent permission survives.

Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â Keep provider adapters behind a common run/session model

The current VS Code language-model API is the only implemented route. Future external coding-agent support should use a protocol adapter such as ACP only after public API/version, transport, lifecycle, authentication and permissions are verified. Adapter output maps to normalized progress, tool request, permission request, file-change proposal, cancellation and result events. No adapter may bypass diff review or approval. **Rollback:** stop adapter process/session and discard un-applied proposals.

Prototype a CRDT/OT-backed editor adapter in an isolated spike; compare undo, local unsaved buffers, permissions, persistence, file size, encoding, EOL, undo semantics and recovery. Yjs is a candidate, not a selected dependency. **Rollback:** preserve local text documents and disable shared mode; never auto-overwrite on reconnect.

Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â Build remote services as separate trust boundaries

Managed identity, room service, WebRTC relay, remote/mobile approvals and cloud execution need separate services, threat models, retention and operational readiness. Do not expose the current extension-host listener as a production internet service. **Rollback:** close room sessions, revoke issued credentials and disable remote endpoint; local solo IDE remains usable.

## C. Skills sourcing report (snapshot checked 21 October 2026)

| Source | Finding | Product implication / risk |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Agent Skills specification](https://agentskills.io/specification) and [canonical repository spec](https://github.com/agentskills/agentskills/blob/main/docs/specification.mdx) | A skill is a directory containing `SKILL.md`; required frontmatter includes a short `name` and non-empty `description`, with optional license and other metadata. Discovery can use only name/description before loading instructions. Optional scripts, references and assets are allowed. | Adopt portable folder and metadata format. Validate names, description length, directory boundaries and file sizes. Optional scripts are executable code and must not run on skill selection. Local ecosystem/provider support still needs compatibility tests. |
| [ACP v1 overview](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/overview), [initialization](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/initialization), [stdio transport](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/transports), and [tool permissions](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/tool-calls) | ACP v1 defines JSON-RPC initialization/session/prompt, newline-delimited stdio, progress notifications, permission requests and cancellation. ACP client capabilities do not sandbox the agent process. | Team Lobby 0.77.0 implements one-shot Ask/Plan ACP v1 with user-selected agent-managed authentication; permission requests are rejected and no filesystem/terminal client methods are advertised. The configured process still runs with the user's OS privileges and environment. |
| [MCP authorization specification](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/blob/main/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/authorization.mdx) | HTTP authorization is optional at protocol level; when used, the cited spec uses OAuth discovery/protected-resource metadata requirements. Transport-specific cases have separate guidance. | An MCP connection does not mean a trusted tool or safe action. Require server identity, user consent, scoped token storage, tool provenance and per-call policy. Recheck current spec before implementation. |
| [VS Code extension capabilities](https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-capabilities/overview) and [webview security](https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-guides/webview) | Extensions can contribute commands, settings, views and webviews; they run in an extension host and cannot access the workbench DOM. Webviews need narrow local roots, CSP and limited capabilities. | Morph should be settings/theme/layout profiles and supported views, not unsupported CSS injection. Widgets need a stronger isolated runtime than a normal privileged extension host. |
| [Zed collaboration overview](https://zed.dev/docs/collaboration/overview) | Current docs describe multiplayer editing, persistent channels, shared project access, voice, and require sign-in; project sharing grants filesystem access within the project. | Room + call + edits already exist elsewhere. Forge differentiation must be accountable human/agent coordination and task/evidence flow. Reuse the lesson: explain precise filesystem-sharing scope.
| [Yjs docs](https://docs.yjs.dev/) | Yjs is an editor-agnostic CRDT with network provider and persistence choices; its docs state shared backend operation/scale requires careful design. | Candidate for a controlled editing spike only. CRDT does not solve room authorization, file-system scope, secret redaction, or user-visible recovery semantics.

The available local skills catalog contains general UX/accessibility and AI-input/tuner/governor/trust guidance. Team Lobby 0.38.0 introduced explicit workspace-local Agent Skills discovery; protocol v10 in 0.39.0 added server-side task ownership checks; protocol v11 in 0.41.0 adds task-linked Build lifecycle activity, and 0.43.0 adds user-approved verification command activity; 0.43.1 redacts common credentials before room sharing. Repository-local `AGENTS.md` and `.forge-room/instructions.md` remain separate project guidance sources.

## D. Vertical-slice sequence

| Milestone | Slice | Exit evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | Extension protocol and test baseline | Existing room behavior, source/test map, package/hash sync | Existing source and unit-test baseline; runtime multi-client checks remain outstanding |
| 1 | Safe code-anchored comment | Explicitly share a bounded comment attached to selected workspace file and line range; receiving client can navigate to the anchor; content remains local; protocol validation and tests | Implemented and helper-tested; multi-client runtime still needed |
| 2 | Two-client room correctness | Two isolated extension hosts; presence, invite pinning/revoke, duplicate-name-safe direct messages, reconnect, task/message/history sync, malformed/unauthorized request rejection | Not runtime-tested |
| 3 | Revoke and audit | Host-controlled invite revoke, server-side deny after revoke, host-attributed event without secret | Invite revocation implemented and helper-tested; independent per-resource grants and two-client runtime validation remain missing |
| 4 | Skills-aware AI run | Standards-validated, user-selected skill metadata/instructions; context provenance; no script execution; provider-backed acceptance | Discovery, metadata validation, explicit selection, transfer disclosure, and no-execution boundary implemented in 0.38.0; helper tests pass; provider-backed UI/runtime acceptance remains outstanding |
| 5 | ACP agent adapter + isolated task run | Version-pinned adapter, session lifecycle, restricted tools, worktree, cancel and evidence-linked diff | 0.77.0 implements fake-agent-tested one-shot Ask/Plan lifecycle, agent-managed authentication choice, denial and cancel. Real-agent acceptance, OS isolation, terminal authentication, persistence and Build integration remain missing. |
| 6 | Shared editing research spike | Compare a CRDT and OT option with test matrix before selecting; preserve local buffers | Missing |
| 7 | Voice/session service | Separate consented voice + follow/inspect scopes; call diagnostics and revocation | Missing |
| 8 | Declarative Morph and working widget/pack | Preview/apply/undo supported settings and a sandboxed, data-bound widget/pack | Missing |
| 9 | Authenticated remote service and Pocket | Online identity, room auth, mobile scoped approvals, offline fail-closed | Missing |

The prompt's V1/V2/V3 groupings are planning labels, not a claim that all listed features can safely ship together. Remote execution, shared editing, calls and a mobile control plane depend on real service and permission infrastructure, so they are not simulated in a desktop-only extension.

## Release note - Team Lobby 0.42.0

Task-linked Build applied events now include up to four bounded relative paths only when the existing workspace file-activity setting is enabled. Event text is capped at 500 characters. Without opt-in, the event retains only the changed-file count and checkpoint ID. The extension test suite passes 30/30 and VSIX packaging succeeds. Runtime validation with two clients and a configured model remains open. SHA-256: d6be44c0beafd68106bd0b021c33d0ae24bef0ca02cf4636fe1295a3452268bc.

## Release note - Team Lobby 0.43.0

Verification command sharing closes part of the AI task visibility gap. It requires a workspace opt-in plus a per-run share choice; the exact command appears in the confirmation first. Commands are capped at 220 characters and must be single-line. The event contains the task, verification name, command and exit result; the Build review bundle includes the command only when it was explicitly shared. Unit tests pass 32/32; end-to-end provider and two-client checks remain open. Package SHA-256: 2a587805ab7ca310779665a1cafaa3084847ba4f86facbb338f87f2b8e4fd14b.

## Release note - Team Lobby 0.43.1

Credential masking now runs before an explicitly shared verification command is added to room activity or checkpoint evidence. It covers common secret flags and environment variable names, bearer/basic values, URL credentials, and common provider-key formats. 33/33 tests pass; end-to-end runtime acceptance remains open. SHA-256: f0c89c11cf71b12dbea809ad6b0d22c04c270b8299ff4c1c638863fbd2c97f5b.

## Release note - Team Lobby 0.44.0

Tasks now support optional validated deadlines and the room displays a live countdown or overdue label; the protocol carries this as an optional task field. Deadline parsing, UTC normalization, invalid input rejection, server-side shape validation, room rendering and ordering are covered by focused tests. Two-client runtime behavior remains unverified. SHA-256: e511b1f79cda450048789c38e9e48415767426ef731ef8f89fc988f12b5e4662.


## Release note - Team Lobby 0.45.0

Rooms now support an optional host-controlled hackathon submission deadline. The normalized UTC deadline is persisted in host-local room history, sent to newly joined participants and broadcast on changes; members can view a live remaining/expired label. Hosts can change or clear it. The deadline parser and host/participant rendering are covered by focused tests. Multi-client runtime behavior, reconnect clock skew and deadline pause semantics remain unverified or unimplemented. 39/39 tests passed. SHA-256: 41c51aed9aa404a99348b4b9adb79d7f6f03acf27f2a6334ca36ae825be727d8.


## Release note - Team Lobby 0.46.0

Before generation, Ask/Plan/Build requests explicit consent for the selected provider's token-count API, reports estimated serialized prompt tokens against the model limit, and asks separately before generation. Failed, invalid and over-limit estimates prevent generation. Tool results are recounted on later turns; provider-side tool overhead may not be included. The budget helper is covered by two focused tests; provider-backed UI flow has not been exercised in the IDE. 41/41 focused tests pass. SHA-256: 4874b27e47e4cf5b3db2fae7d55e81b38b2cbc70fc71b75378840ee9260ff97c.

## Release note - Team Lobby 0.47.0

AI context selection now hides likely credential and private key paths by default. Users explicitly reveal candidate paths and confirm the chosen sensitive files before local read, with the existing provider transfer confirmation still required. This heuristic can miss secrets and may classify ordinary source filenames as sensitive. Path-classifier tests pass; picker behavior needs IDE runtime validation. 43/43 focused tests pass. SHA-256: 2fb404cd73dfec817f32a64f64c5997a75e0f7bf625f1c40c04aef3db280d410.



## Release note - Team Lobby 0.48.0

Workspace search tools now omit likely credential/private-key paths. A direct model request to read a likely sensitive file requires a separate path-specific confirmation even when request-wide read-only tools were approved; denials are remembered for the rest of the request. The API contract and classifier compile and focused path tests pass, but provider-backed tool execution has not been exercised in the IDE. Detection remains heuristic and cannot guarantee secret discovery. 43/43 tests pass. SHA-256: 150eac446b2e012a9cfa832890784a40d001cfbd1006d7ed43ed3402d471e2a0.

## Master-prompt audit addendum - 21 October 2026

The attached `Forge_AI_Native_Expansion_Master_Prompt.txt` is product and engineering guidance, not evidence that its requested systems exist. Its V0 audit, V1-V3 roadmap, security controls, and required first tested milestone are reconciled here. Existing implementation remains an extension-based Code OSS fork, not a greenfield native agent runtime.

### Architecture decisions

- **ADR-01 - Extend Code OSS through supported APIs first.** Forge Studio and Team Lobby use built-in extension contributions. Prompted customization should use theme/profile/layout/settings APIs; extensions cannot access the workbench DOM or inject supported global styles. Full control over workbench internals requires deliberate fork-level engineering and regression maintenance. Official references: https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-capabilities/overview and https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-capabilities/extending-workbench.
- **ADR-02 - Keep model, tool, skill, and room permissions separate.** A selected skill is untrusted guidance and never grants a capability. Provider/model access is user-selected; workspace tool calls and file mutations require distinct approvals; room sharing is an independent consent boundary.
- **ADR-03 - Land lifecycle control before autonomous execution.** Pause/resume/discard must preserve or erase state deterministically before adding provider adapters, shell tools, or remote dispatch. Build pause never applies an incomplete proposal.
- **ADR-04 - Defer ACP/MCP adapters until capability mapping exists.** ACP is the candidate agent-session protocol; MCP is the candidate tool/resource protocol. Neither substitutes for Forge authorization. Protocol/version/capability negotiation and per-operation grants are prerequisites. References: https://agentclientprotocol.com/ and https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/authorization.
- **ADR-05 - Do not represent non-existent remote infrastructure as implemented.** Voice, shared editing, Mesh/Pipeline, Pocket, and hosted identities require service and security architecture; no mock UI will be counted as delivery.

### Prioritized implementation sequence

1. **Agent lifecycle controls**: tested active pause, resumable session, and discard behavior. Implemented in Team Lobby 0.49.0; provider-backed IDE exercise remains required.
2. **Two-client correctness and privacy**: isolated host/client validation for invites, revocation, presence, task authorization, reconnect, and bounded shared artifacts.
3. **Skills provenance and operational use**: extend existing SKILL.md validation with source/version/license/requirements and provider compatibility records; never execute skill scripts on selection.
4. **Native capability API and agent adapter**: Team Lobby 0.70–0.76 provides a typed read-only workspace registry and one-shot ACP v1 Ask/Plan process adapter. Next: real-agent UI acceptance, stronger OS process isolation, and separately reviewed authentication/session persistence before Build or client-side tools.
5. **Collaboration primitives**: evaluate working-tree snapshots, follow/spectate, controlled shared edit, voice and service boundaries independently; do not conflate periodic file activity with live buffers.
6. **Declarative Morph and local Pack**: supported workbench settings/themes/layout previews and undo first; export/import only already implemented components with schema validation.
7. **Widgets/Workshop sandbox**: capability-bound data, restrictive webview/runtime, resource limits, preview, revoke, uninstall and rollback.
8. **Mesh/Pipeline and Pocket**: only after authenticated identities, durable events, scoped authorization, reconnect, isolation, and test evidence are available.

### 0.49.0 milestone evidence

Team Lobby now contributes separate Pause Active Request, Resume Paused Session, and Discard Last Agent Session commands. Pause cancels the active provider call while retaining bounded session state and partial response; restart recovery uses the existing opt-in persistence. Resume uses the saved model flow and transfer confirmation. Discard cancels in-flight generation and clears both saved transcript types only after generation ends, preventing the canceled request from recreating discarded state. Build pauses record a pause outcome and apply no proposed edits. Helper state tests and the existing suite pass 45/45. No model is configured in this development profile, so provider-stream behavior and resume-after-restart still need IDE runtime validation.
### Distribution and extension boundary check

The current product registers built-in VSIX files in the local Code OSS fork; it does not configure a VS Code Marketplace gallery (`extensionsGallery` is null). No public listing or distribution claim is made by this milestone. Code - OSS is the open-source upstream product with its own branding and marketplace constraints; any future public distribution must separately review the repository's LICENSE, Microsoft Marketplace terms, product branding and included extension licenses. The audited extension surface permits contributed themes, views, commands, settings and webviews but blocks extension access to workbench DOM/custom global CSS. That limits Morph to supported customization APIs unless a separately maintained fork-level implementation is chosen.
## Release note - Team Lobby 0.50.0

Task dependencies now affect the host-authoritative state machine. Starting or completing a dependent task is rejected until its prerequisites are done. The dependency picker prevents introducing unfinished prerequisites to active tasks; the status picker and Build/worktree task selectors surface the block reason. Reopening a prerequisite is rejected while a direct or transitive dependent is active or done. This is a local-room workflow rule, not an automatic scheduler: it does not dispatch work or infer dependencies. TypeScript build and 48/48 focused tests pass; two-client UI/runtime validation remains outstanding.
## Release note - Team Lobby 0.51.0

For tasks with acceptance criteria, moving to Done now requires a persisted owner attestation for every distinct criterion. The host validates remote changes; a completed task must be reopened before its criteria or attestations can change. Cards show which criteria were owner-confirmed. This remains a human assertion and is explicitly distinct from test/verification events. Legacy duplicate criterion text remains readable; new duplicate entries are rejected. TypeScript build and 52/52 focused tests pass; multi-client UI runtime validation remains open.
## Release note - Team Lobby 0.52.0

The local member card now uses the stable participant ID from the current host or join identity rather than a synthetic `self` value. This fixes self-assignment and reassignment: the submitted task owner ID matches the host's authorization membership map. Direct-message recipient selection also excludes the current participant by stable ID. Existing saved host tasks and room event snapshots with legacy owner ID `self` are remapped on load. Helper and webview tests pass 54/54; two-client LAN runtime validation remains outstanding.
## Release note — Team Lobby 0.61.0

Read-only workspace tool calls in Build now add bounded source provenance to the applied checkpoint and Markdown review export. The evidence records tool name, workspace-relative source paths, returned character count, outcome, and timestamp; search arguments and result text are excluded. Twenty-entry and field validation guards apply to both collection and restore. Four helper tests cover extraction, content/query exclusion, outcomes, and bounds. Team Lobby 0.61.0 builds and passes 70/70 focused extension tests. Provider-backed operation and review-bundle UI validation remain open.

## Release note — Team Lobby 0.62.0

Added reusable agent profiles in workspace-local .forge-room/agent-profiles.json, an editor command to create/open the file, and optional per-request profile selection for Ask, Plan, and Build. Profile text is untrusted guidance, receives no tool authority, and is disclosed with name and character count in transfer consent and token estimation. Strict size, schema, field, and duplicate-name validation is covered by three tests. Build and 73/73 focused tests pass; provider-backed runtime validation remains open.


## Master prompt review and implementation status — 21 October 2026

The supplied Forge_AI_Native_Expansion_Master_Prompt.txt is a product requirements and implementation handoff. Its complete feature inventory is not a claim that each future capability is approved for immediate release or already exists. The existing audit and milestone map are the repository-grounded response to its inspection-first requirements. Current implementation remains incremental: room presence, activity, task flow, code comments, diff review, invite revocation, and local multi-client transport have source and automated coverage; calls, shared editing, remote control/mobile, ACP execution, widget sandboxing, Workshop, and Forge Packs remain future work requiring separate architecture and runtime validation.

This pass ships the bounded reusable-profile slice in Team Lobby 0.62.0. Profiles are selected per Ask/Plan/Build request, treated as untrusted guidance, included in transfer/token receipts, and cannot grant capabilities. Workspace profile creation refuses an existing .forge-room directory that resolves outside the project. Build and 73/73 focused tests pass in both extension copies. The first mandatory two-client acceptance suite is still only partly proven at extension UI level; local transport tests do not substitute for multi-window IDE acceptance.


## Release note — Team Lobby 0.63.0

Implemented nested AGENTS.md discovery as optional folder-scoped guidance in Ask, Plan, and Build. The user explicitly chooses sources; paths are resolved inside the workspace and sensitive paths retain their additional approval. Discovery skips dependency/build directories and context is bounded to ten files, 12,000 characters per file and 48,000 total. New destination resolution rejects escaping parents and symbolic-link targets for project-instruction and profile starter files. Build and 76/76 tests pass in handoff and Code OSS copies; provider-backed interaction still needs manual validation. Package SHA-256: C1393B84F7BDDFF31915CC8917D308FD32E33222A2EA5FD8A22D2DBC77F63487.

Completion details reuse content-free tool evidence: tool, outcome, up to three source paths and returned character count. No search query or result text appears in progress. TypeScript check and 77/77 tests pass; provider UI runtime validation remains open. Package SHA-256: 32B8DF297BC16F7B2FCB3A11D5BE884FD298A34B7D617CD556BA5B0D2742FAF6.


## Release note — Team Lobby 0.65.0

Build lifecycle and verification events now include a validated 	askId; host-side policy permits links only for the assigned owner or host. The versioned room protocol is 12. The local multi-client transport test verifies an owner can publish a linked Build event and a different participant cannot. TypeScript check and 78/78 tests pass. Provider UI and multi-window runtime validation remain open. Package SHA-256: C3472A7ACE776D3DCF690491FB25D2A70DEC293D88C9391758222ED9F7871649.

## Team Lobby 0.66.0 — task-owned agent presence

A new or resumed task-linked Build now appears in the room as a separate virtual agent participant, with the selected model label, owning human participant ID, task ID, and working/review status. Presence is sent in room protocol 13, is accepted by the host only when the connected participant owns the linked task, is cleared when the Build exits, and is included in room history/count snapshots for reconnecting clients. Agent cards resolve their task through the owner ID. This does not create a remote agent runtime or grant the agent separate workspace permissions. TypeScript check and all 78 focused tests pass in the Code OSS extension; handoff mirror and VSIX package are synchronized. Provider-backed and multi-window IDE flows remain unverified. VSIX SHA-256: `8a82682364c55aff36ff79bc518c7cc1df25bccb51f928fcd896a83177e6a8db`.

## Master expansion handoff status

The supplied Forge AI Native Expansion master prompt is product direction, not code. Current repo coverage includes Forge Studio appearance profiles and themes, Team Lobby tasks/chat/activity, task-linked agent workflows, guarded context/tools, and local transport tests. The prompt's remaining platform-scale areas include a native capability catalog, real external agent adapters, voice calls, live shared editing/follow mode, mobile participation, sandboxed widget/workshop execution, and portable Forge Packs. These require separate verified vertical slices and runtime acceptance; they are not represented as complete by this release.
## Team Lobby 0.67.0 — task-linked room reviews

Review summaries can now optionally reference a room task. The host confirms the task exists, permits any authenticated room participant to post the review, and stores the task ID with the event. The roster task owner does not change. Task-linked agent and verification events retain their stricter owner-or-host rule. Protocol 14 distinguishes this expanded event contract. TypeScript check and 78/78 focused tests pass in both extension copies; local multi-client transport coverage verifies reviewers can link a task and missing task IDs are rejected. VSIX package SHA-256 is recorded in product.json.
## Team Lobby 0.68.0 — remaining-risk review evidence

Build review export now asks for an optional user-entered note describing remaining risks or open questions (up to 2,000 characters). The note is local to the exported Markdown bundle, is displayed as quoted text, and is labeled as unverified. Canceling the prompt cancels export. Three focused tests cover empty notes, Markdown line handling, control-character removal, and size limits. Both extension copies pass TypeScript checking and 81/81 focused tests. Provider-backed Build and IDE UI flow still require hands-on validation. The VSIX package hash is registered in product.json.

Forge now has its first native read-only capability registry slice: search and file reads are typed, input-validated, workspace-scoped, approval-gated and logged with content-free outcome metadata. The provider tool loop cannot execute an unregistered call. TypeScript check passes and 88/88 tests pass. This does not yet implement a general capability catalog, write/command capabilities, cross-agent execution or provider-backed runtime validation. ## Current evidence — Team Lobby 0.71.0 multiplayer slice

Room diff/comment validation is now shared between the extension host and transport tests. The two-client suite verifies event delivery, actor/time metadata, history replay, invite revocation, active peer disconnect and post-revoke denial without secret leakage. This improves automated V1 evidence but does not establish two separate IDE clients or multi-machine/TLS behavior. Extension check passes and 91/91 tests pass. Package SHA-256: `3F82E2B8610C1E9002B7F35E513D7251389FDBBEDD6175DF3FD72D4BF2AD98F0`.

## Team Lobby 0.73.0 — explicit editor location sharing

Added **Team Lobby: Share Current Editor Location**. The user confirms each share. The room event carries only a workspace-relative file path and one-based line number, not selection text or file contents. The host validates the path and line before persisting and broadcasting the event. A teammate can open the event to navigate to that line in their own workspace; missing files and out-of-range lines are handled locally. This is a one-time navigation handoff, not live cursor tracking or shared editing.

**Verification:** `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 94/94 tests in both the Code OSS extension and handoff mirror; `node --check media/room.js` passes. The new path validator rejects traversal, absolute paths, control characters, and invalid line numbers. VSIX SHA-256: `F05899D3F70388BE1592D52F0CCAE925590EDBA8180DAFEAF976E456AE456EDC`. Two-IDE UI acceptance remains open.

## Team Lobby 0.74.0 — opt-in live editor follow

Added **Team Lobby: Toggle Live Editor Location Sharing**, **Team Lobby: Follow Teammate**, and **Team Lobby: Stop Following Teammate**. Sharing requires a modal confirmation, streams only an in-workspace relative file path and one-based line number, and is limited to four updates per second. It sends no selection, buffer content, or cursor text and remains ephemeral rather than entering room history. Participants can stop sharing or stop following independently. Follow navigation resolves paths inside the receiver's own workspace and clamps stale line numbers. Reconnect history contains the current locations only for participants still actively sharing; disconnect, invite revocation, and explicit stop clear the host's live location state.

**Verification:** `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 97/97 tests in both the Code OSS extension and handoff mirror; `node --check media/room.js` passes. The new two-client transport test covers location delivery, invalid path rejection, stop-sharing removal, and exclusion from persisted events; helper tests cover the four-per-second throttle and strict payload reduction. VSIX SHA-256: `7AEA38B1D8A98D49ECF05128BCE102387ACE784B9A16F1C3B37A7CB8A2E6D74B`. Separate IDE follow behavior remains to be exercised. This provides spectate/follow at file-and-line level; shared editing and shared buffer synchronization remain unimplemented.

## Team Lobby 0.75.0 - read-only Git status for AI

Registered `git_status` as a typed workspace-scoped capability alongside search and named-file read. It uses the built-in Git extension API and reports branch name, staged/modified/untracked/conflict counts, and up to 40 non-sensitive paths relative to the selected workspace. It reads no diffs or file contents and runs no shell command. Existing per-call or request-scoped approval, capability audit, output budgeting, and content-free evidence apply.

**Verification:** `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 100/100 tests in both the Code OSS extension and handoff mirror. The capability registry rejects arguments for Git status; formatter tests cover the 40-path cap, sensitive-path omission, unrelated repositories, and nested workspaces; evidence tests confirm branch and result text are not persisted. Separate IDE/provider acceptance remains open. This completes only the read-only Git status capability; external ACP adapters and write/command capabilities remain open.
VSIX SHA-256: `988E532B5455651CB2CEC19E417638C2EB222738D34A2F2548E83FE9F6B23089`.

## Team Lobby 0.76.0 - external ACP Ask and Plan

Added optional ACP v1 as a provider choice for Ask and Plan. A machine-scoped executable and argument list launch through direct stdio without a shell. Forge Room negotiates protocol version 1, creates a one-shot session with no MCP servers, streams agent text, supports cancellation, advertises no filesystem or terminal client methods, and rejects session permission requests. External Build is not enabled; native Build remains on the IDE Language Model API path with Forge Room's reviewed diff/apply flow.

Before transfer, a modal confirmation shows the exact executable/arguments, workspace, and selected-context summary. It states that the local process runs with the user's OS account and environment and may read or change files, execute commands, or access the network outside Forge Room's client permissions. ACP sessions are not resumable and token/cost estimation is unavailable.

**Verification:** fake ACP process tests cover v1 negotiation, one-shot session/prompt, text streaming, no advertised client tools/MCP servers, permission denial, cancellation, unsupported protocol rejection, and shell-free command validation. `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 104/104 tests in both Code OSS and handoff copies. Real-agent/manual UI acceptance and OS process isolation remain open.
VSIX SHA-256: `04BF33E7A850B8AA1D675164D714D0147841817C94F833A26EB35FF2777D2F49`.

## Team Lobby 0.77.0 - ACP agent sign-in

ACP v1 Ask/Plan now handles agents that advertise in-agent authentication methods. After the configured process starts, Forge Room offers the supported agent-managed sign-in choices by name and description; the selected method is sent using ACP `authenticate`. Canceling the chooser stops the request before a session is created. Terminal-auth methods remain unavailable because Forge Room does not provide an interactive terminal login flow. The existing consent warns that the external process has the user's OS privileges and environment; ACP method selection does not sandbox it.

Research: [ACP v1 initialization](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/initialization) defines advertised auth methods and client capabilities; [ACP v1 authentication](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/authentication) defines agent-managed `authenticate` and terminal-auth requirements. Checked 21 October 2026.

**Verification:** `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 106/106 tests in both Code OSS and handoff copies. Fake-agent tests cover the offered method filter, chosen method ID, and canceled login. Real-agent/manual IDE acceptance and OS process isolation remain open.
VSIX SHA-256: `74EF40D8570683FA83019C3CC7EB0DB00A5E59373EFB2BE090A5CDAEE6EB0815`.


## Team Lobby 0.78.0 - assigned Git worktree verification

Task-linked Build and Build resume now require an exact Git identity match: the active workspace root must match a registered or discovered linked worktree in the same repository, its actual branch must match the branch recorded for the local path, and both must match the room task's assigned branch. This rejects stale records that point to another branch, the main checkout, or a worktree in another repository. Filesystem path comparison is case-insensitive on Windows and case-sensitive on POSIX.

A new integration test creates and commits a temporary repository, adds a linked worktree in a path containing spaces, reads the real Git root/branch/git-dir/common-dir values, and verifies correct identity plus wrong-task-branch, wrong-checked-out-branch, and main-worktree rejection.

**Verification:** `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 108/108 tests in both Code OSS and handoff copies. `@vscode/vsce package --no-dependencies` succeeds; package SHA-256: `A02CB58F0A29AABC02D93BA0B51F38EBC408F3A85558D22BAE7219E9C471595E`. Full IDE Build selection and resume UI flows still need runtime acceptance.


## Team Lobby 0.79.0 - stale assigned worktree recovery

When a cached local worktree record is absent, stale, or points to a branch other than the task's assigned branch, Forge Room now checks Git's registered worktrees for that exact task branch. The candidate must still pass actual root, repository common-directory, checked-out branch, and linked-worktree identity checks before its device-local path mapping is refreshed. This lets another IDE window or a recovered workspace find an existing valid worktree rather than attempting to add a branch that Git already has checked out.

**Verification:** `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 108/108 tests in both Code OSS and handoff copies. The Git integration test uses a real temporary repository and linked worktree with a path containing spaces, then verifies matching and mismatched branches plus worktree discovery. `@vscode/vsce package --no-dependencies` succeeds; package SHA-256: `BF22296F3965C8CFFA528091A8A142917B5F9F56FE2F329818F82836BE14D13C`. Full IDE startup/recovery UX remains to validate.


## Team Lobby 0.80.0 - integrated task worktree recovery

Task worktree validation and rediscovery now run through one production resolver. It checks cached records against the actual Git root, repository common directory, checked-out branch and linked-worktree metadata. When a cached record is stale, it searches Git's registered worktrees for the task's exact assigned branch, validates that candidate through the same checks, and refreshes the device-local path mapping. A different repository with a coincidentally identical branch is rejected.

The Git integration test creates a temporary repository and linked worktree, then calls the production resolver with a stale path, a valid cached path, and a task branch that is not present. It verifies recovery, cache retention, and fail-closed missing-branch behavior.

**Verification:** `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 108/108 tests in both Code OSS and handoff copies. `@vscode/vsce package --no-dependencies` succeeds; package SHA-256: `C6165382FF41927C08A856B9CCCEEEDA142CBE9675C1552F19323AD29CC7AA56`. Full IDE Build selection/resume interaction remains to validate.


## Team Lobby 0.81.0 - safe local task worktree cleanup

Added the **Team Lobby: Remove Clean Local Task Worktree** command to close the local task worktree lifecycle. It only offers worktrees verified against the task branch and current repository. It refuses to remove the workspace currently open in the IDE, checks for tracked and untracked changes, and requires modal confirmation showing the exact folder and deletion effect. After confirmation it revalidates the same worktree and calls `git worktree remove` without `--force`; Git can reject a change that occurs after the inspection. The shared task assignment and local Git branch are preserved. No file paths, contents, or room events are sent by cleanup.

A real Git integration test proves dirty files prevent removal and remain present, while a clean reviewed worktree is removed and its branch remains. **Verification:** `npm.cmd run check` passes and `npm.cmd test` passes 109/109 tests in both Code OSS and handoff copies. `@vscode/vsce package --no-dependencies` succeeds; package SHA-256: `646E955E42FAFAF3A8AC85F2178AC214A4C1BC4E2CBDCE7AA99C728002073D5B`. IDE command-palette interaction remains to validate.

## Team Lobby 0.82.0 - live shared document implementation

Added the host-started, participant-requested and host-approved shared document flow, backed by Yjs and VS Code document edits. A text file is shared in memory up to 512 KB; state transfers only to approved subscribers, and local buffers remain unsaved. Reconnect requires host approval and merges offline CRDT state. Protocol 15 was required for the initial single-root implementation. Source research and constraints are documented in [AI_CORE_FEATURE_RESEARCH.md](../../docs/research/AI_CORE_FEATURE_RESEARCH.md).

**Verification:** Both extension copies pass `npm.cmd run check` and 115/115 tests. The bundled VSIX was packaged without `node_modules`; its SHA-256 matches the built-in Code OSS manifest: `4902A209CCE3487A4DBC9932D36EE1188207C77378D4152A31CACE5EE65B4F51`. Separate IDE editing/reconnect acceptance remains open.

## Team Lobby 0.83.0 - multi-root shared documents

Shared documents can now be selected from any uniquely named workspace root. Multi-root room paths include the root name, duplicate names are rejected, and joining clients resolve files under the matching root with filesystem containment checks. Protocol 16 is required. Both copies pass TypeScript checks and 116/116 tests. The bundled VSIX hash matches the built-in manifest. Two-IDE editing and reconnect acceptance remain open.
# Current state note (21 October 2026)

This file contains earlier audit snapshots and historical implementation decisions. For the current repository audit, gap matrix, skills report, sourced standards review, milestone gates, and 0.85.0 evidence, use [FORGE_AI_NATIVE_MASTER_AUDIT.md](FORGE_AI_NATIVE_MASTER_AUDIT.md). Earlier version-specific statements below are historical and are superseded by that snapshot.
